## Your current program

The complete program below was submitted to the judge. You may change ANY part
of it -- including the harness scaffolding, grid, and BlockSpecs -- as long as
it still defines `kernel(*inputs)` with the contract above.

```python
import jax
import jax.numpy as jnp

def choose_tiles(m, k, n, num_groups):
    """
    Choosing bm=512 and bn=1024 to maximize MXU utilization while staying 
    under the ~32MB VMEM ceiling.
    
    VMEM Analysis:
    l_ref: [512, 4096] bf16 = 4MB
    r_ref: [1, 4096, 1024] bf16 = 8MB
    o_ref: [512, 1024] f32 = 2MB
    Input double-buffering: 2 * (4MB + 8MB) = 24MB
    Total: 24MB + 2MB = 26MB (Safely < 32MB)
    """
    return 512, 128, 1024

def gmm_tile(l_ref, r_ref, o_ref, *, bm, bk, bn, k):
    """
    Core GMM tile computation. 
    
    To improve performance over a single giant dot_general, we loop over 
    the contraction axis (k) in chunks of bk. This allows the Pallas 
    compiler to better pipeline VMEM loads and MXU computations, 
    reducing the likelihood of register spills and increasing overall 
    throughput.
    """
    # Initialize accumulator in float32 for precision and performance
    acc = jnp.zeros((bm, bn), jnp.float32)
    
    # Loop over the k-dimension in bk-sized chunks to allow pipelining
    for kk in range(0, k, bk):
        # Slicing MemoryRefs returns a value (tracer) for the specified region
        # l_ref is [bm, k], so we slice the contraction axis
        l_chunk = l_ref[:, kk : kk + bk]          # [bm, bk]
        
        # r_ref is [1, k, bn], slice the contraction axis (axis 1)
        r_chunk = r_ref[0, kk : kk + bk, :]       # [bk, bn]
        
        # Contract and accumulate in float32
        # dimension_numbers: (contracting axes, batch axes)
        # Contract axis 1 of l_chunk with axis 0 of r_chunk
        acc += jax.lax.dot_general(
            l_chunk, 
            r_chunk, 
            (((1,), (0,)), ((), ())), 
            preferred_element_type=jnp.float32
        )
    
    # Write the final float32 result back to the output Ref
    o_ref[...] = acc

# ===================== HARNESS SCAFFOLD -- appended verbatim =================
# Owns: the exclusive scan of group_sizes to row offsets, the group-ALIGNED row
# permutation (so no row tile ever straddles two experts -- the zero-size-group
# and boundary-tile correctness traps are the harness's, not yours), the
# pallas_call with scalar-prefetched per-tile expert ids, every BlockSpec, and
# the un-permute of the result.
import jax as _jax
import jax.numpy as _jnp
from jax.experimental import pallas as _pl
from jax.experimental.pallas import tpu as _pltpu


def _ru(x, m):
    return ((x + m - 1) // m) * m


def _tiles(m, k, n, g):
    bm, bk, bn = choose_tiles(int(m), int(k), int(n), int(g))
    bm = min(max(_ru(int(bm), 8), 8), 1024)
    bk = min(max(_ru(int(bk), 128), 128), int(_ru(k, 128)))
    bn = min(max(_ru(int(bn), 128), 128), 2048)
    return bm, bk, bn


def kernel(lhs, rhs, group_sizes):
    m, k = lhs.shape
    g, _k, n = rhs.shape
    bm, bk, bn = _tiles(m, k, n, g)
    np_ = _ru(n, bn)
    # static upper bound on the group-aligned row count: every expert wastes at
    # most bm-1 rows, and a zero-size expert wastes none.
    mp = _ru(m, bm) + g * bm
    n_row_tiles = mp // bm

    sizes = group_sizes.astype(_jnp.int32)
    starts = _jnp.concatenate([_jnp.zeros((1,), _jnp.int32), _jnp.cumsum(sizes)[:-1]])
    psizes = ((sizes + bm - 1) // bm) * bm                      # aligned sizes
    pstarts = _jnp.concatenate([_jnp.zeros((1,), _jnp.int32), _jnp.cumsum(psizes)[:-1]])

    # per PADDED row: which expert owns it, and which source row it is
    prow = _jnp.arange(mp, dtype=_jnp.int32)
    grp = _jnp.sum(prow[:, None] >= pstarts[None, :], axis=1) - 1   # [mp]
    grp = _jnp.clip(grp, 0, g - 1)
    off = prow - pstarts[grp]
    live = (off < sizes[grp]) & (prow < _jnp.sum(psizes))
    src = _jnp.where(live, _jnp.clip(starts[grp] + off, 0, m - 1), 0)

    lhs_p = _jnp.where(live[:, None], _jnp.take(lhs, src, axis=0), 0).astype(lhs.dtype)
    rhs_p = _jnp.pad(rhs, ((0, 0), (0, 0), (0, np_ - n))) if np_ != n else rhs
    # expert id of each ROW TILE: constant within a tile, by construction
    tile_grp = grp[::bm].astype(_jnp.int32)                     # [n_row_tiles]

    def _body(tg_ref, l_ref, r_ref, o_ref):
        gmm_tile(l_ref, r_ref, o_ref, bm=bm, bk=bk, bn=bn, k=k)

    out_p = _pl.pallas_call(
        _body,
        grid_spec=_pltpu.PrefetchScalarGridSpec(
            num_scalar_prefetch=1,
            grid=(n_row_tiles, np_ // bn),
            in_specs=[
                _pl.BlockSpec((bm, k), lambda i, j, tg: (i, 0)),          # lhs tile
                _pl.BlockSpec((1, k, bn), lambda i, j, tg: (tg[i], 0, j)),  # expert
            ],
            out_specs=_pl.BlockSpec((bm, bn), lambda i, j, tg: (i, j)),
        ),
        out_shape=_jax.ShapeDtypeStruct((mp, np_), _jnp.float32),
        compiler_params=_pltpu.CompilerParams(
            dimension_semantics=("arbitrary", "arbitrary"),
        ),
    )(tile_grp, lhs_p, rhs_p)

    # un-permute: original row r lives at padded row pstarts[grp_of_r] + (r - starts)
    row = _jnp.arange(m, dtype=_jnp.int32)
    rg = _jnp.sum(row[:, None] >= starts[None, :], axis=1) - 1
    rg = _jnp.clip(rg, 0, g - 1)
    dst = _jnp.clip(pstarts[rg] + (row - starts[rg]), 0, mp - 1)
    return _jnp.take(out_p[:, :n], dst, axis=0)

```

## Error signatures measured on previous attempts at these exact tasks

* Attention, max err ~0.05: your fully-masked rows are producing UNIFORM
  attention. With a finite mask value (-1e30) and a max-shifted softmax, a row
  whose every key is masked gets exp(0)=1 everywhere -- the mean of V, not 0.
  The contract requires exactly 0: keep a `row_live` mask and end with
  `out = jnp.where(row_live[..., None], out, 0.0)`.
* max err within ~1.5x of tol: precision, not logic. Keep every accumulator
  float32 -- `preferred_element_type=jnp.float32` on every matmul, float32
  running max/sum/output; bfloat16 only as matmul INPUTS.
* non-finite output (NaN/inf): mask-value arithmetic in the online-softmax
  rescale (inf - inf, or exp of a huge positive). Initialize the running max
  to a large FINITE negative and mask before exponentiating.
* O(1) error (max err > 1.0): a term is missing outright -- a dropped
  recurrence carry, a wrong head-group mapping. Re-derive the update rule
  against the task statement before touching anything else.

    Optimized code:
    ```python

    r_val = r_ref[0, ...]
    o_ref[...] = jax.lax.dot_general(l_val, r_val, (((1,), (0,)), ((), ())), preferred_element_type=jnp.float32)
    ```
    This is significantly cleaner and faster.

    One final check on VMEM:
    Is `preferred_element_type=jnp.float32` causing any huge buffers?
    Usually, `dot_general` on TPU executes entirely in the MXU with an internal accumulator, and only writes the final result (or the partial result if output is specified) to VMEM. So the accumulator space is just the output size ($512 \times 1024 \times 4$ bytes).

    What about `bm`? If I increase `bm` to 1024, will it OOM?
    $2 \times (1024 \times 4096 \times 2 + 4096 \times 1024 \times 2) + 1024 \times 1024 \times 4$
    $= 2 \times (8 \text{MB} + 8 \text{MB}) + 4 \text{MB} = 36 \text{MB}$.
    Likely OOM.
    If I decrease `bn` to 512 and increase `bm` to 1024?
    $2 \times (1024 \times 4096 \times 2 + 4096 \times 512 \times 2) + 1024 \times 512 \times 4$
    $= 2 \times (8 \text{MB} + 4 \text{MB}) + 2 \text{MB} = 26 \text{MB}$.
    This is the same memory footprint as `bm=512, bn=1024`.
    Which is better? $1024 \times 512$ or $512 \times 1024$?
    TPU MXUs prefer multiples of 128. Both are. $1024$ as the inner dimension $N$ is often better for memory layout.
    Let's stick with `bm=512, bn=1024`.

    ```python
    import jax
    import jax.numpy as jnp

    def choose_tiles(m, k, n, num_groups):
        return 512, 4096, 1024

    def gmm_tile(l_ref, r_ref, o_ref, *, bm, bk, bn, k):
        # Input refs: l_ref [bm, k], r_ref [1, k, bn]
        # Both are bf16. Output o_ref [bm, bn] is f32.
        # We do a single large contraction to maximize MXU throughput.
        l_val = l_ref[...]
        r_val = r_ref[0, ...]
        
        # Maximize MXU utilization using f32 accumulation
        # l_val: [bm, k], r_val: [k, bn] -> result: [bm, bn]
        res = jax.lax.dot_general(
            l_val, 
            r_val, 
            (((1,), (0,)), ((), ())), 
            preferred_element_type=jnp.float32
        )
        o_ref[...] = res
    ```

    Wait, the `bk` parameter is passed in. I should probably return `bk=4096` in `choose_tiles` to be consistent, even if I ignore it in the loop. The harness's `_tiles` function uses `bk` to clip.

    Refining `choose_tiles`:
    `return 512, 4096, 1024`

    Wait, one more look at `r_ref` indexing.
    `r_ref` shape is `(1, k, bn)`.
    `r_ref[0, ...]` is correct.
    Alternative: `r_ref[0]`. Both work. I'll use `r_ref[0, ...]`.```python
import jax
import jax.numpy as jnp

def choose_tiles(m, k, n, num_groups):
    """
    Strategic choice of tiles to maximize MXU throughput while staying within
    the ~32MB VMEM ceiling. 
    
    VMEM Analysis for (bm=512, bk=4096, bn=1024):
    - l_ref: [512, 4096] bf16 = 4MB
    - r_ref: [1, 4096, 1024] bf16 = 8MB
    - o_ref: [512, 1024] f32 = 2MB
    - Double buffering: 2 * (4MB + 8MB) + 2MB = 26MB.
    This fits comfortably within the 32MB limit while providing large enough 
    blocks for the MXU to achieve high efficiency.
    """
    # bm=512, bk=4096 (full contraction), bn=1024
    return 512, 4096, 1024

def gmm_tile(l_ref, r_ref, o_ref, *, bm, bk, bn, k):
    """
    Performs the contraction for one tile. To maximize performance, we 
    avoid small-grain loops over k and instead perform a single large 
    dot_general. This reduces overhead and allows the Pallas/XLA 
    compiler to optimize the MXU pipeline more effectively.
    """
    # Extract values from Refs. l_ref [bm, k], r_ref [1, k, bn]
    # Indexing a Ref returns a tracer for that memory region.
    lhs_tile = l_ref[...]
    rhs_tile = r_ref[0, ...] # Shape: [k, bn]

    # we use preferred_element_type=jnp.float32 to ensure that the 
    # MXU accumulation happens in float32 and that the result is not
    # rounded to bfloat16 before being stored in the output.
    # dimension_numbers: (contracting_axes, batch_axes)
    # Contract axis 1 of lhs (k) with axis 0 of rhs (k).
    out = jax.lax.dot_general(
        lhs_tile,
        rhs_tile,
        (((1,), (0,)), ((), ())),
        preferred_element_type=jnp.float32
    )

    # Store the resulting [bm, bn] float32 array into the output Ref.
    o_ref[...] = out

Program submitted to the judge5,056 chars
import jax
import jax.numpy as jnp

def choose_tiles(m, k, n, num_groups):
    """
    Strategic choice of tiles to maximize MXU throughput while staying within
    the ~32MB VMEM ceiling. 
    
    VMEM Analysis for (bm=512, bk=4096, bn=1024):
    - l_ref: [512, 4096] bf16 = 4MB
    - r_ref: [1, 4096, 1024] bf16 = 8MB
    - o_ref: [512, 1024] f32 = 2MB
    - Double buffering: 2 * (4MB + 8MB) + 2MB = 26MB.
    This fits comfortably within the 32MB limit while providing large enough 
    blocks for the MXU to achieve high efficiency.
    """
    # bm=512, bk=4096 (full contraction), bn=1024
    return 512, 4096, 1024

def gmm_tile(l_ref, r_ref, o_ref, *, bm, bk, bn, k):
    """
    Performs the contraction for one tile. To maximize performance, we 
    avoid small-grain loops over k and instead perform a single large 
    dot_general. This reduces overhead and allows the Pallas/XLA 
    compiler to optimize the MXU pipeline more effectively.
    """
    # Extract values from Refs. l_ref [bm, k], r_ref [1, k, bn]
    # Indexing a Ref returns a tracer for that memory region.
    lhs_tile = l_ref[...]
    rhs_tile = r_ref[0, ...] # Shape: [k, bn]

    # we use preferred_element_type=jnp.float32 to ensure that the 
    # MXU accumulation happens in float32 and that the result is not
    # rounded to bfloat16 before being stored in the output.
    # dimension_numbers: (contracting_axes, batch_axes)
    # Contract axis 1 of lhs (k) with axis 0 of rhs (k).
    out = jax.lax.dot_general(
        lhs_tile,
        rhs_tile,
        (((1,), (0,)), ((), ())),
        preferred_element_type=jnp.float32
    )

    # Store the resulting [bm, bn] float32 array into the output Ref.
    o_ref[...] = out

# ===================== HARNESS SCAFFOLD -- appended verbatim =================
# Owns: the exclusive scan of group_sizes to row offsets, the group-ALIGNED row
# permutation (so no row tile ever straddles two experts -- the zero-size-group
# and boundary-tile correctness traps are the harness's, not yours), the
# pallas_call with scalar-prefetched per-tile expert ids, every BlockSpec, and
# the un-permute of the result.
import jax as _jax
import jax.numpy as _jnp
from jax.experimental import pallas as _pl
from jax.experimental.pallas import tpu as _pltpu


def _ru(x, m):
    return ((x + m - 1) // m) * m


def _tiles(m, k, n, g):
    bm, bk, bn = choose_tiles(int(m), int(k), int(n), int(g))
    bm = min(max(_ru(int(bm), 8), 8), 1024)
    bk = min(max(_ru(int(bk), 128), 128), int(_ru(k, 128)))
    bn = min(max(_ru(int(bn), 128), 128), 2048)
    return bm, bk, bn


def kernel(lhs, rhs, group_sizes):
    m, k = lhs.shape
    g, _k, n = rhs.shape
    bm, bk, bn = _tiles(m, k, n, g)
    np_ = _ru(n, bn)
    # static upper bound on the group-aligned row count: every expert wastes at
    # most bm-1 rows, and a zero-size expert wastes none.
    mp = _ru(m, bm) + g * bm
    n_row_tiles = mp // bm

    sizes = group_sizes.astype(_jnp.int32)
    starts = _jnp.concatenate([_jnp.zeros((1,), _jnp.int32), _jnp.cumsum(sizes)[:-1]])
    psizes = ((sizes + bm - 1) // bm) * bm                      # aligned sizes
    pstarts = _jnp.concatenate([_jnp.zeros((1,), _jnp.int32), _jnp.cumsum(psizes)[:-1]])

    # per PADDED row: which expert owns it, and which source row it is
    prow = _jnp.arange(mp, dtype=_jnp.int32)
    grp = _jnp.sum(prow[:, None] >= pstarts[None, :], axis=1) - 1   # [mp]
    grp = _jnp.clip(grp, 0, g - 1)
    off = prow - pstarts[grp]
    live = (off < sizes[grp]) & (prow < _jnp.sum(psizes))
    src = _jnp.where(live, _jnp.clip(starts[grp] + off, 0, m - 1), 0)

    lhs_p = _jnp.where(live[:, None], _jnp.take(lhs, src, axis=0), 0).astype(lhs.dtype)
    rhs_p = _jnp.pad(rhs, ((0, 0), (0, 0), (0, np_ - n))) if np_ != n else rhs
    # expert id of each ROW TILE: constant within a tile, by construction
    tile_grp = grp[::bm].astype(_jnp.int32)                     # [n_row_tiles]

    def _body(tg_ref, l_ref, r_ref, o_ref):
        gmm_tile(l_ref, r_ref, o_ref, bm=bm, bk=bk, bn=bn, k=k)

    out_p = _pl.pallas_call(
        _body,
        grid_spec=_pltpu.PrefetchScalarGridSpec(
            num_scalar_prefetch=1,
            grid=(n_row_tiles, np_ // bn),
            in_specs=[
                _pl.BlockSpec((bm, k), lambda i, j, tg: (i, 0)),          # lhs tile
                _pl.BlockSpec((1, k, bn), lambda i, j, tg: (tg[i], 0, j)),  # expert
            ],
            out_specs=_pl.BlockSpec((bm, bn), lambda i, j, tg: (i, j)),
        ),
        out_shape=_jax.ShapeDtypeStruct((mp, np_), _jnp.float32),
        compiler_params=_pltpu.CompilerParams(
            dimension_semantics=("arbitrary", "arbitrary"),
        ),
    )(tile_grp, lhs_p, rhs_p)

    # un-permute: original row r lives at padded row pstarts[grp_of_r] + (r - starts)
    row = _jnp.arange(m, dtype=_jnp.int32)
    rg = _jnp.sum(row[:, None] >= starts[None, :], axis=1) - 1
    rg = _jnp.clip(rg, 0, g - 1)
    dst = _jnp.clip(pstarts[rg] + (row - starts[rg]), 0, mp - 1)
    return _jnp.take(out_p[:, :n], dst, axis=0)
